Let me think about this problem. We have STLC with locally nameless binders, call-by-value. Need strong normalization.

Standard approach: logical relation via a "hereditarily strongly normalizing" / reducibility candidate approach.

Since we need strong normalization (every reduction path finite), a standard approach is to define a logical predicate/relation `R T t` which is:
- For Bool: `strongly_normalizing t`
- For arrow T1 -> T2: `strongly_normalizing t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)` — but this naive definition doesn't directly work for proving the fundamental theorem under CBV because application of a non-value to an argument gets stuck? Actually in CBV with a strongly normalizing relation, the standard "hereditarily strongly normalizing" relation works:

Define `SN t := strongly_normalizing t`. Then:
- `R Bool t := SN t`
- `R (T1 -> T2) t := SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u)`.

For the fundamental theorem, in the lambda case we need to show `R (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 t1)` given that `R T2 (open t1 v)` for all `v` with `R T1 v` under extended context... Hmm, but the typing rule uses opening with a free variable, and then substitution. We need a lemma about substituting a strongly normalizing term for a free variable preserves strong normalization. That is the crux.

Alternative approaches:
1. Use a logical relation with explicit substitutions/environments: `R T t` defined with the typing context? Actually the standard Tait-style proof for STLC with named variables and CBV uses reducibility candidates:
   - `SN t` for base
   - For arrow: `SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)`.
   Under call-by-name this is fine: `app t u` steps when t is an abstraction eventually. But under CBV, if `t` is a closed strongly normalizing term of function type, it's a value or reduces to one... Hmm, the key issue: for `t` strongly normalizing but not a value, `app t u` might get stuck? No—if `t` is well-typed closed of arrow type and SN, then by progress, either t is a value (abstraction) or it steps. If t reduces to t' then app t u steps to app t' u. So it's fine, but we need progress. Actually the definition of R needs to be defined only on well-typed terms in the fundamental theorem, so we can incorporate typing or design R such that it works.

Let me think about the cleanest formalization that will actually compile.

Standard approach in Software Foundations (StlcProp / Norm.v) uses a "strongly normalizing" inductive with `sn` and defines `R T t := SN t /\ ...`. But for CBV there's the issue that non-values are SN but applying them is not directly reducible via the relation.

In general, the point: `SN` is closed under application in CBV? I think there's a known result: in a "value-calculus" with a well-behaved language, the property holds: if t1 and t2 are SN then app t1 t2 is SN. Intuitively, reductions of app t1 t2 are interleavings of reductions in t1 until it becomes a value (or stuck forever - impossible since SN means it must terminate), then t2 until value, then beta. Hmm — but the subtlety: `ST_App1` requires `locally_closed t2`. If t2 is not locally closed, app t1 t2 might get stuck even though t1 steps — then app t1 t2 has fewer reductions. Fewer reductions means more likely SN. So the "stuck" case is not dangerous. The dangerous case would be if app had MORE reductions than the components. Reductions of app t1 t2 only occur via subterms or beta, and beta reduces the app. So if t1, t2 SN, then app t1 t2 SN? Let's attempt a formal proof:

We want: SN t1 -> SN t2 -> SN (app t1 t2). Prove by induction on SN t1, then SN t2 (nested). Consider any step app t1 t2 --> r. Cases:
- ST_AppAbs: t1 = abs T t, v = t2 value, r = open t t2. Hmm, need SN (open t t2) — that's a substitution lemma not available structurally! So this general statement is hard: it requires the substitution lemma for SN.

So indeed we need the substitution lemma: SN t (under fvar x?) Hmm.

Hmm, that requires a logical relation that's "substitutive" — you can prove by a semantic argument: R is closed under substitution of related terms. ```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=

  | Ty_Bool => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.
```

Hmm. But wait: maybe in this setting all relevant terms are locally closed? In the fundamental theorem we handle closed terms (empty context). Option A: Semantic typing with valuations.

Define:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => strongly_normalizing t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u)
  end.
```

Note: no lc requirement. Then fundamental theorem: forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, (forall x T', Gamma x = Some T' -> R T' (sigma x)) -> R T (subst_fvar sigma t)? where subst_fvar is a function atom -> tm substitution.

Case T_Var: Gamma x = Some T, sigma x is R T. subst gives sigma x. Done.

Case T_Abs: has_type Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2), with hypothesis H: forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (x|->T1;Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
Need R (T1->T2) (subst_fvar sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) = R (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst_fvar sigma t1)) — need a lemma that subst under binders commutes; subst_fvar doesn't touch bvars so it just recurses: subst_fvar sigma (tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (subst_fvar sigma t1). Trivial.

Need SN (tm_abs T1 (subst_fvar sigma t1)) — that's true if it's locally closed? SN (abs ...) requires no steps at all: abs never steps regardless of lc (step doesn't apply to abs as source). Well, SN is defined inductively with a single constructor: SN t iff all t' with t-->t' are SN. If t has no steps, SN t holds trivially (well-founded: SN_intro with the forall hypothesis empty). Since `step` has no rule with conclusion `abs ... --> _`, abs is SN always. So SN (abs ...) holds regardless.

Need forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u).

Hmm. Here we have the app of the substituted abs with u. The body is subst_fvar sigma t1. Hmm, careful: subst_fvar sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) where sigma' substitutes sigma for other fvars and u for x. Let me think about the actual mathematical structure carefully and design a proof that goes through.

Setting: CBV STLC. We want SN for closed well-typed terms.

Classical proof (e.g., from "Reducibility candidates" adapted to CBV, as in the literature on "strong normalization of call-by-value"): The reducibility candidate for CBV is defined as:

R Bool := SN
R (A->B) := { t | SN t ∧ ∀u. R A u ⇒ R B (t u) }.

Key lemmas:
1. R A t ⇒ SN t.
2. R A t ⇒ SN t (and maybe: t --> t' ⇒ R A t').
3. If SN t and all t' with t --> t' satisfy R A t' then R A t. (expansion)
4. For locally closed abs: if ∀u. R A u ⇒ R B (open body u), then R (A->B) (abs A body).

For (4), we need (3) plus handling beta. For (3), T = A->B, t arbitrary SN with all reducts in R(A->B). Need forall u ∈ R A, R B (t u). Prove by induction on SN t and SN u (lexicographic pair? or nested induction on SN t then SN u). Reducts of t u: 
 - t --> t', u arbitrary: if lc u (true when u related? we need lc for ST_App1), app t' u ∈ R B by hypothesis R (A->B) t' and R A u. 
 - t is a value (abs or true/false; if t = true/false then app is stuck, no App2), u --> u': app t u' — need induction hypothesis with SN u' and same SN t (t not reduced): we need R B (t u') — by IH on SN u since u' is a reduct, and we need to know that t satisfies "all reducts in R" (unchanged) and SN t. So nested induction: outer on SN t, inner on SN u. Then app t u' satisfies IH.
 - beta: t = abs A body (a value), u = a value, app --> open body u. This is the problematic case: the reduct is not of the form app t' u''. We need R B (open body u) directly. In case (3), the hypothesis "all reducts of t are in R(A->B)" doesn't tell us anything about body-vs-u interaction. So (3) is false in this naive form? Let's test: t = abs A body where body = app (bvar 0) (bvar 0)?? Let t = λx. x x. Is t SN? Yes (abs). All reducts of t: none. So hypothesis vacuous. R (A->B) t would require forall u ∈ R A, R B ((λx.xx) u). But (λx.xx) u with u ∈ R A: if u = true (A=B=Bool?), R Bool (app true true) = SN (app true true) = true. If A = B->C and u ∈ R (B->C) is a well-behaved function, then (λx. x x) u steps to u u. But u x requires... u is a function in R, so R C (u u)? Requires R B u, i.e., u ∈ R B and u ∈ R (B->C) i.e. B = B->C (infinite type), impossible for simple types. So typing saves us, but statement (3) without typing is false. Hence all these lemmas need typing hypotheses! I.e., the logical relation arguments must be done for *well-typed* terms, and R A t should perhaps include well-typedness.

That's the standard approach: define R A t together with typing, i.e., prove the fundamental theorem by induction on the typing derivation and use the typing in each case. But the lemmas like expansion (3) need typing: t has type A and all reducts are in R A. Hmm, in case (3) with beta, we know t = abs A body has type A->B and all reducts (none) in R; but to show forall u ∈ R A, R B (app t u), we need to handle u ∈ R A where u is typed A? R A u gives SN but does it give typing? If we define R A t to include has_type? But with what context? This gets into the semantic typing judgment design. 

OK here's the cleaner architecture that I'm confident about: Define semantic typing as a judgment on terms with a context, using valuations:

`Gamma ⊨ t : T` := forall sigma, (forall x T', Gamma x = Some T' -> R T' (sigma x)) -> R T (subst sigma t).

Where `R` is as above (SN-based). R does not include typing. Then the fundamental theorem is proven by induction on the typing derivation. The induction gives us strong IH on subderivations, which is what makes the beta case work: in the abs case, the IH gives for every v ∈ R T1, R T2 (open t1 v), and then we prove R T2 (app (abs t1) u) for u ∈ R T1 using *that* uniform hypothesis. This works if we prove the abs-app lemma using the uniform hypothesis plus the typing of t1? Hmm, do we need typing of the body? Let's see what's available: In the T_Abs case, the IH (from the premise derivation, which is a subderivation after applying T_Abs's hypothesis to a chosen x) is exactly the semantic typing of `open t1 (fvar x)` in context x|->T1;Gamma, which yields the uniform statement for u ∈ R T1: R T2 (open t1' u) where t1' = subst sigma t1. So the uniform hypothesis is available.

Now the abs-app lemma: lc_at 1 t (locally closed abstraction) + (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u). Do we need typing of t? Let's try to prove it by induction on the structure of T2 and the SN of u, without typing. The untyped counterexample above: t = λx. x x has type (B->C)->? no: λx. x x would need x : A and x : A->B, untypable. But in our lemma the hypothesis is (forall v ∈ R T1, R T2 (open t v)), which cannot be satisfied by λx.xx when T1 is finite type because open t v = v v and R T2 (v v) requires v ∈ R T1 and v ∈ R (T1->T2)? Hmm, R T2 (v v) does not presuppose typing; it might be false for type reasons: if T2 = Bool, R Bool (v v) = SN (v v). Is v v SN for v ∈ R T1? If T1 is an arrow type A->B and T2 arbitrary... For v ∈ R T1 = R (A->B), we have SN v and closure. SN (v v) requires R B (v v)? No, it just requires SN. Hmm, if v is a "function" in R (A->B) and T1 = A->B, then v v requires R A v, which isn't implied. So could v v loop? v ∈ R (A->B) and v applied to itself: R (A->B) v gives forall u ∈ R A, R B (v u). If v ∈ R A doesn't hold, we can't conclude. But the hypothesis forall v' ∈ R T1, R T2 (open t v') with open t v' = v' v' means for v' = v: R T2 (v v). So if the hypothesis holds for t = λx.xx, then for all v ∈ R T1, R T2 (v v), so in particular R Bool (v v) = SN (v v). Interesting — so the hypothesis is strong and might make the proof go through untyped!

Let's test the abs-app lemma for T2 = Bool with t = λx. x x, T1 = Bool: hypothesis forall v ∈ R Bool (=SN), SN (v v)?? But v = true: v v = true true is stuck, SN. v = λx.true: SN (abs applied to itself) → beta to true, SN. Hmm, hard to violate. Since the hypothesis is very strong (it asserts SN of open t v for ALL related v), maybe the lemma provable untyped. But wait, the hypothesis says R T2 (open t v); if T2 is arrow, that includes SN plus closure under apps. Very strong.

Let's try to prove the abs-app lemma by induction on the SN derivation of u and on T2 structure. Hmm, let me think about the actual proof strategy more carefully, and consider proving a more general lemma:

Lemma (main): For all T, R T is "downward closed under steps" (CR2) and satisfies "beta/expansion": 
(a) R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
(b) For locally closed t (maybe all t?), (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u).

Let's try proving (a) and (b) simultaneously by induction on T? They involve T1, T2 subtypes, so induction on T seems plausible: to prove (b) for T2 = T1' -> T2', we need (a) and (b) for T1' and T2'. OK so here's the thing: the semantic proof fundamentally requires typing to handle the beta case, because R at arrow types only constrains applications, not bodies. In the fundamental theorem, the abs case gets H2 (the uniform body-relatedness) from the IH, which is exactly the missing info. I think the fundamental issue is that R at arrow needs to be defined in a way that's *stable under reduction* including to abstractions, and the natural fix is to define R coinductively-ish or via "t is SN and every reduct of t that is a value (abs) is related". Let's think about the "reducibility candidates à la Girard for CBV" more carefully. In Girard's method for call-by-name, candidates are sets of SN terms closed under reduction and with a "neutral" closure condition: if t is neutral and all its reducts are in the candidate then t is in the candidate. For CBV, the analogue of neutral is "not a value" (sometimes called "stuck"?). Let's define:

A set S of lc terms is a CBV-candidate if:
(C1) S ⊆ SN.
(C2) S is closed under step.
(C3) If t is not a value, lc t, SN? and for all t' with t --> t', t' ∈ S, then t ∈ S. (This is "expansion at non-values".)

Hmm, for a non-value t, app t u behaves: its reducts reduce the function or the argument, and no beta can occur at the top since the function is not a value... wait, if t is not a value, app t u's function is t; beta needs t = abs (a value). So no top-level beta. Good. So candidates at arrow types could be defined with this neutral rule. Then V (A->B) (abs body) needs a separate condition relating the body to arguments.

Actually, here's the standard definition of reducibility for CBV I now recall from the literature (e.g., in the proof of SN for the CBV STLC in "Semantics with Applications"? or in Andrew Pitts' notes?): 

- `Red A` for terms, `Red_v A` for values.
- `Red Bool t := SN t`
- `Red (A->B) t := SN t ∧ ∀ t', t -->* t' → value t' → Red_v (A->B) t'`
- `Red_v (A->B) (λx.body) := ∀ u, Red A u → Red B (body[u/x])`
Then one proves: t ∈ Red (A->B) iff SN t and for all u ∈ Red A, t u ∈ Red B. The "iff" direction requires: given t ∈ Red (A->B) and u ∈ Red A, show t u ∈ Red B: SN (t u) and all value-reducts of t u are in Red_v B. Reducts of t u: since t is SN, t -->* v (a value) — does SN t and well-typedness imply t reduces to a value? For well-typed t yes (progress + SN). For untyped, no (fvar x). Hmm, so Red A must also imply "t reduces to a value" — but for fvar x this fails. So the relation Red must either include typing or be restricted to terms that are "well-typed/closed enough".

Alternatively define Red to include a "semantic value-ness": every SN term... no.

For this recursive call to have the same shape, we need t not a value (so app t u's head is a non-value) OR at least t not an abs. So the right induction invariant is: t is not a value! Because if t is not a value, app t u has head t which is not a value. C. The fundamental theorem:

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, (forall x T', Gamma x = Some T' -> R T' (sigma x)) ->
  R T (subst_fvar sigma t).
```

Proof by induction on the typing derivation. Cases:
- T_Var: Gamma x = Some T. subst gives sigma x. Apply Hsigma. Need `subst_fvar sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x` ✓ by definition/simpl.
- T_True/T_False: R Bool tm_true = lc ∧ SN. lc ✓ (lc_at 0 true). SN: SN_intro, no steps from true? Is `true` a value; can `true --> t'`? No step rule has true as source. ✓.
- T_App: Gamma ⊢ t1 : T1->T2, t2 : T1. IH1: forall sigma, valuation → R (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1). IH2: R T1 (subst sigma t2). Then R T2 (app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)) = R T2 (subst sigma (app t1 t2)) ✓ by definition of the forall-clause and subst commuting with app.
- T_Abs: as described. Need R (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
   * lc: from lc_open_inv + identity, as described.
   * SN: abs has no steps → SN ✓.
   * forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) u): use R_abs_app with t := subst sigma t1, H1 := lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1), H2 := forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open (subst sigma t1) v), H3 := u related.
     H2 from IH: for each v ∈ R T1, apply IH with sigma' = extend x v sigma, use identity (needs x ∉ fvars t1), valuation condition ✓.

Wait: in T_Abs the IH is about `has_type (x|->T1;Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`, and I want to apply it with a valuation sigma' that extends sigma at x with v. And x ∉ fvars t1 and x ∉ L: choose fresh.

So the key ingredients are:

Let me now define R concretely:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Projections: `R_lc : R T t -> locally_closed t` by destruct T; simpl; tauto. `R_sn : R T t -> SN t`.

CR (R_step): R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof by induction on T (generalizing t t').
- Bool: destruct R as [lc sn]; split: lc_step; SN step.
- Arrow: R = [lc [sn H]]. Need lc t', SN t', forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t' u).
  - lc t' by lc_step.
  - SN t' by SN_step.
  - for u: need R T2 (app t' u). We have H u : R T2 (app t u). Apply IH (for T2) to app t u with step app t u --> app t' u, requiring locally_closed u (ST_App1) — get from R T1 u. ✓.
  Note the IH is for T2, a subterm. Good, induction on T with IH for T1 and T2.

Need to set up: `induction T; intros t t' HR Hstep; simpl in *; ...`. Hmm, T1, T2 are variables in the arrow case; using `induction T` gives IH for T1 and T2? In Coq, `induction T` on `ty` gives induction hypotheses for the recursive arguments T1, T2 as `IHT1`, `IHT2` if the goal is generalized over t t'. Yes.

lc_step: locally_closed t -> step t t' -> locally_closed t'.
- ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T body) v, t' = open body v. Need lc_at 0 of open body v from lc_at 1 body and lc_at 0 v. With the lemma `lc_at_open_rec : lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)` ✓ (need to prove).
- ST_App1: lc (app t1 t2) gives lc t1, lc t2; IH gives lc t1'; lc (app t1' t2) ✓.
- ST_App2: similar.

SN_step: SN t -> t --> t' -> SN t'. By induction on the SN derivation: SN_intro t H where H : forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'. Apply H to the step. Actually `induction 1` etc.

Wait: is SN_step needed as a separate lemma, or does CR at Bool need it? Yes.

Hmm, one important check: `strongly_normalizing` here is defined inductively (an inductive predicate with the "all reducts SN" constructor). So SN t is *equivalent* to well-foundedness but is an inductive predicate, and proving SN by SN_intro is fine. SN_step: induction on SN t. If SN t is built by SN_intro t H, then H t' Hstep gives SN t'. So `induction HSN` and apply. Fine.

Now nonvalue_expansion. Let me state it carefully:

```
Lemma R_nonvalue : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  ~ value t ->
  R T t.
```

Proof by induction on T; intros t lc sn hall nv; simpl.
- Ty_Bool: split; assumption.
- Ty_Arrow T1 T2: split; [assumption| split; [assumption|]]. intros u Hu.
  Apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u).
  * lc: lc_app lc (R_lc Hu).
  * SN (app t u): apply SN_intro; intros r Hr; inversion Hr; subst.
    - ST_AppAbs case: t = abs T0 body, value v... but t is not a value, contradiction with nv (value (tm_abs ...)). 
    - ST_App1: r = app t1' t2 with t1 = t and t2 = u and t --> t1', H : step t t1' → hall t1' H : R (T1->T2) t1', hence R T2 (app t1' u) — wait, `hall t1' H` gives R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1', and the forall clause with u gives R T2 (app t1' u) = R T2 r. Then R_sn. ✓
    - ST_App2: requires value t1 = t — contradiction nv.
  * hall': intros r Hr; inversion Hr; subst;
    - App1: as above, R T2 (app t1' u) from hall.
    - AppAbs: contradiction (value t).
    - App2: value t contradiction.
  * ~ value (app t u): intro; inversion H. — value is only abs/true/false, not app. Prove a lemma `value_app_absurd`.
  
  Wait, careful: the statement of the induction hypothesis IHT2: forall t, lc t -> SN t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> R T2 t') -> ~ value t -> R T2 t. Good.

Hmm, note that in the arrow case, we don't need R at T1 at all! Interesting — the lemma only recurses into T2. So actually the induction is on T2's structure; T1 irrelevant. Fine.

Now R_abs_app:

```
Lemma R_abs_app : forall T1 T2 t u,
  lc_at 1 t ->
  (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) ->
  R T1 u ->
  R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 t) u).
```

Proof by well-founded induction on SN u? We have R_sn Hu : SN u. So `remember (R_sn Hu)` or use `induction (R_sn Hu)` after generalizing? The statement for fixed T1 T2 t and H1, H2, and u varying. Standard approach: revert dependent stuff; we can use `induction Hsn` where Hsn : SN u, generalizing u? Since SN is an inductive predicate whose constructor's premise quantifies over reducts, `induction Hsn` gives IH: forall u' (step u u'), R_abs_app T1 T2 t u' (with the other hypotheses...). But the other hypotheses H1, H2 are fixed, and H3 : R T1 u varies with u. Let's set it up as:

```
intros T1 T2 t u Hlc Hbody Hu.
remember (R_sn Hu) as Hsn eqn:Eq.
revert u Hu Eq.
induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH]; intros u Hu Eq.
```
Hmm, the standard pattern: `induction Hsn` with Hsn : SN u. Since SN u's constructor is `SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t-->t' -> SN t') -> SN t`. To induct, generalize the goal over the term: `remember (R_sn Hu) as Hsn` then `revert u Hu Hsn Eq`? Let's think: we want to prove P(u) := R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u) for all u with SN u, by induction on SN u. The clean way:

```
Lemma R_abs_app_aux : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t ->
   (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) ->
   R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u).
Proof.
  induction 1 as [u Hreds IH].
  intros T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody Hu.
  ...
Qed.
```
Wait, when we do `induction 1` on SN u with everything else universally quantified in the goal (T1 T2 t are after the SN hypothesis in the statement? The statement starts with `forall u, SN u -> forall T1 T2 t, ...` so when doing induction on the SN hypothesis, the trailing intros (T1 T2 t, etc.) haven't been introduced yet, so the IH will be for all of them. Let me order the quantifiers: `forall u, SN u -> forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u)`. Then `induction 1` — hmm, induction on a hypothesis that has the form SN u where u is a variable bound before... `induction 1` when goal is `SN u -> ...`? Let me just write:

Proof with `intros u Hsn; induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH]; intros T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody Hu.` Hmm, after `intros u Hsn`, Hsn : SN u. `induction Hsn` — Coq generalizes u? The induction principle for strongly_normalizing: `strongly_normalizing_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop, (forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t`. With Hsn : SN u, `induction Hsn` would need P such that P u is the goal; but the goal depends on u and other hyps. Coq's `induction Hsn` typically fails if u appears in the goal but isn't generalized... Actually `induction Hsn` when Hsn : SN u and u occurs in the goal: Coq will generalize the goal over u, giving P := fun u => goal-with-u. Then the IH gives P t' for reducts. That's exactly what we want: P u := forall T1 T2 t ..., R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u). Then IH : forall t', u --> t' -> P t'. Good. But the other hypotheses (T1 T2 t, Hlc, Hbody, Hu) are in the goal context... if we do `intros` of them before induction, they become fixed and the goal depends on them; Coq's induction would generalize over u but keep the others fixed, yielding IH : forall t', u --> t' -> (forall T1 T2 t, ...) — hmm, only if they were universally quantified in the goal. If T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody Hu are in the context as fixed variables, the induction principle instantiated with P := fun x => R T2 (app (abs T1 t) x) gives IH : forall t', step u t' -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) t'). That's still fine! Because Hlc, Hbody are fixed and the needed IH for u' is exactly R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u') with the same T1, t. But we also need Hu' : R T1 u' for the recursive call — that's derived from CR Hu (u --> u'). ✓. So actually the induction works with fixed T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody. But careful: the induction hypothesis is only about the same T2 etc. That's all we need. Good.

5. Substitution identity: `subst_fvar (extend sigma x u) (open t (fvar x)) = open (subst_fvar sigma t) u` when x ∉ fvars t.

   Let me define:
   ```
   Fixpoint subst_fvar (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
     match t with
     | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
     | tm_fvar x => sigma x
     | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_fvar sigma t1) (subst_fvar sigma t2)
     | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_fvar sigma t1)
     | tm_true => tm_true
     | tm_false => tm_false
     end.
   Definition extend (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
     fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
   ```

Actually `induction Hsn` uses the induction scheme for strongly_normalizing:
```
strongly_normalizing_ind : forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t : tm, (forall t' : tm, step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t : tm, strongly_normalizing t -> P t
```
To use, Coq needs to find P such that P u matches the goal. Since the goal is `forall T1 T2 t, ...` with u free, Coq abstracts u in the goal: P := fun x => forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> ... -> R T1 x -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) x). Then the IH becomes: forall t', step u t' -> P t' — exactly what we need (including the quantified T1 T2 t etc.). ✓. So ordering the statement with SN before the rest is good.

```coq
(** * Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof *)

(* Free variables of a term. *)
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  end.

Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atom xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atom l).

Lemma In_max_atom : forall (l : list atom) (x : atom), In x l -> x <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl. destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall (l : list atom), ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (In_max_atom l (S (max_atom l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

(* Simultaneous free-variable substitution. *)
Fixpoint subst_fvar (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x

```coq
(* ---------- Basic facts about steps and values ---------- *)

Lemma step_abs_inv : forall T t t', ~ (tm_abs T t --> t').
Proof. intros T t t' H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_fvar_inv : forall x t', ~ (tm_fvar x --> t').
Proof. intros x t' H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_true_inv : forall t', ~ (tm_true --> t').
Proof. intros t' H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma step_false_inv : forall t', ~ (tm_false --> t').
Proof. intros t' H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma value_app_inv : forall t1 t2, ~ value (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof. intros t1 t2 H. inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_intro' : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t') -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. apply SN_intro. Qed.  (* not needed *)

Lemma SN_no_step : forall t, (forall t', ~ (t --> t')) -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t H. apply SN_intro. intros t' Hs. exact (False_ind _ (H t' Hs)).
Qed.

Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' HSN Hstep. induction HSN as [t Hred]. apply Hred. assumption. Qed.
```
Hmm, `induction HSN as [t Hred]` — HSN : SN t; the constructor SN_intro has args (t) (H). But since t is in the context and the goal is SN t', `induction HSN` might complain. Better:
```
Proof. intros t t' HSN Hstep. induction HSN as [t0 Hred]. simpl. apply Hred. ...
```

```coq
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hlc Hstep. induction Hstep.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_at_abs_inv in H1.
    apply lc_open_rec; assumption.
  - (* ST_App1 *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app; [apply IHHstep; assumption | assumption].
  - (* ST_App2 *) similar.
Qed.
```
Note: locally_closed = lc_at 0. In ST_AppAbs, H1 : lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t), lc_at_abs_inv gives lc_at 1 t. H2 : lc_at 0 v. lc_open_rec with k := 0 ✓.

Now R:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; simpl; intros t H; tauto. Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; simpl; intros t H; tauto. Qed.

Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 t u, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof. intros. simpl in H. tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl in H` on H : R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t should reduce the Fixpoint since the first argument is a constructor ✓. `tauto` extracts the third conjunct. Or `destruct H as [H1 [H2 H3]]; apply H3; assumption`.

Actually wait: inversion on Hr where the *term* is tm_app t u and t is a variable: Coq inversion of an inductive relation with variable arguments can be finicky; it may not substitute t = tm_abs T0 body automatically but produce the equation. Let's use a helper lemma to characterize app steps:

```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t u r,
  tm_app t u --> r ->
  (exists T body v, t = tm_abs T body /\ u = v /\ r = open body v) \/
  (exists t', t --> t' /\ r = tm_app t' u) \/
  (exists u', value t /\ u --> u' /\ r = tm_app t u').
Proof.
  intros t u r H. inversion H as [T0 body v Hlc Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hs Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hs]; subst.
  - left. exists T0, body, v. auto.
  - right; left. exists t1'. auto.
  - right; right. exists t2'. auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `inversion H as [...]` with patterns for three constructors. The constructor argument patterns: ST_AppAbs has args T, t, v, Hlc, Hv (5 args). ST_App1 has t1, t1', t2, Hs, Hlc (5). ST_App2 has v1, t2, t2', Hv, Hs (5). With `inversion H as [T0 body v Hlc Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hs Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hs]` — the number of names per branch must match the constructor's arguments including the ones determined by unification? Inversion will try to substitute; patterns like this usually work with `inversion H as [a b c H1 H2 | ...]`. It may complain about the number of arguments. Alternatively use `inversion H; subst; ...` and then use eauto. Let me try:

```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t u r,
  tm_app t u --> r ->
  (exists T body v, t = tm_abs T body /\ r = open body v) \/

The resulting goal after `induction Hsn as [t Hreds IH]` is typically: `forall t, ...`? Let me recall from practice: with `H : even n` for an inductive Prop with index n, `induction H` gives goals corresponding to each constructor, with n substituted appropriately. E.g., `Inductive even : nat -> Prop := even0 : even 0 | evenS : forall n, even n -> even (S n).` If n is a variable, `induction H` (H : even n) gives:
- case even0: goal P 0 (a separate goal)
- case evenS: goal P (S n0) with IH : P n0.
So it's not a single "forall t" goal; it's a separate goal per constructor with the index unified. Since even0 and evenS have different index shapes, you get multiple goals. For strongly_normalizing with a single constructor `SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t`, induction on Hsn : SN u gives one goal: P u, but with the constructor's argument: t is unified with u (since SN u must match SN t). For the recursive occurrence `SN t'` inside the argument `forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'`, Coq's induction scheme generates the IH under the binder. So yes, `induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH]` gives IH : forall t', u --> t' -> P t'.

Coq's `induction` generalizes dependent occurrences; yes it will create the motive abstracting u. But it may fail if the goal depends on u in a way it can't abstract — here all occurrences of u are as a term argument, so abstraction is fine.

So after `induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH]`, the goal is `forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> ... -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u)` with IH : forall u', u --> u' -> (forall T1 T2 t, ... -> R T1 u' -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u')). Good, that's what we need.

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, valuation Gamma sigma -> R T (subst_fvar sigma t).

```coq
(* ============================================================ *)
(* Auxiliary definitions for the strong normalization proof.     *)
(* ============================================================ *)

(* Free variables of a term (binders are de Bruijn, so every    *)
(* [tm_fvar] occurring anywhere is free).                       *)

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atom xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_atom l).

Lemma In_max_atom : forall (l : list atom) (x : atom),
  In x l -> x <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin.
  - destruct Hin.
  - simpl in Hin. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_atom ys); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  pose proof (In_max_atom l (S (max_atom l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
Fixpoint subst_fvar (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_fvar sigma t1) (subst_fvar sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_fvar sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition extend (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_fvar_id : forall t, subst_fvar tm_fvar t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; try reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hs; simpl in *.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply lc_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Hforall]]. split.
    + apply lc_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + split.
      * apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
      * intros u Hu. apply IH2 with (t := tm_app t u).

Qed.

```coq
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t u r,
  tm_app t u --> r ->
  (exists T body v, t = tm_abs T body /\ value t /\ u = v /\ r = open body v) \/
  (exists t', t --> t' /\ r = tm_app t' u) \/
  (exists u', value t /\ u --> u' /\ r = tm_app t u').
Proof.
  intros t u r H. inversion H as [T0 body v Hlc Hv | t1 t1' t2 Hs Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hv Hs]; subst.
  - left. exists T0, body, v. split; [reflexivity|]. split.
    + apply v_abs. exact Hlc.
    + split; [reflexivity | reflexivity].
  - right. left. exists t1'. split; assumption.
  - right. right. exists t2'. split; [assumption|]. split; [assumption|reflexivity].
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma R_nonvalue : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  ~ value t ->
  R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 T2 IHT2]; intros t Hlc Hsn Hred Hnv; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split; [assumption|]. split; [assumption|].
    intros u Hu.
    apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u).
    + apply lc_app; [assumption | apply R_lc with (T := T1); assumption].
    + apply SN_intro. intros r Hr.
      destruct (step_app_inv _ _ _ Hr) as
        [[T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] | [[t' [Hs Er]] | [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]]]].
      * subst t. exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hv.
      * subst r. apply R_sn with (T := T2).
        pose proof (Hred t' Hs) as HR'. 
        apply R_app with (T1 := T1) in HR'; [exact HR' | exact Hu].
      * subst t. exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hv.
    + intros r Hr.
      destruct (step_app_inv _ _ _ Hr) as
        [[T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] | [[t' [Hs Er]] | [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]]]].
      * subst t. exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hv.
      * subst r. apply R_app with (T1 := T1). apply Hred. exact Hs. exact Hu.
      * subst t. exfalso. apply Hnv. exact Hv.
    + apply value_app_inv.
Qed.

Now R_abs_app_aux:

```coq
Lemma R_abs_app_aux : forall u, strongly_normalizing u ->
  forall T1 T2 (t : tm),
    lc_at 1 t ->
    (forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (open t v)) ->
    R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 t) u).
Proof.
  intros u Hsn. induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH].
  intros T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody Hu.
  apply R_nonvalue with (t := tm_app (tm_abs T1 t) u).
  - apply lc_app. apply lc_abs. exact Hlc. apply R_lc with (T := T1). exact Hu.
  - apply SN_intro. intros r Hr.
    destruct (step_app_inv _ _ _ Hr) as
      [[T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] | [[t' [Hs Er]] | [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]]]].
    + (* AppAbs: tm_abs T1 t = tm_abs T0 body, u = v, r = open body v *)
      subst. apply R_sn with (T := T2). apply Hbody. exact Hu.
    + (* App1: step (tm_abs T1 t) t' -- impossible *)
      subst. exfalso. exact (step_abs_inv T1 t t' Hs).
    + (* App2 *)
      subst r. apply R_sn with (T := T2).
      apply IH with (T1 := T1) (T2 := T2) (t := t).
      * exact Hs.
      * exact Hlc.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply R_step with (t := u). exact Hu. exact Hs.
  - intros r Hr.
    destruct (step_app_inv _ _ _ Hr) as
      [[T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] | [[t' [Hs Er]] | [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]]]].
    + subst. apply Hbody. exact Hu.
    + subst. exfalso. exact (step_abs_inv T1 t t' Hs).
    + subst r. apply IH with (T1 := T1) (T2 := T2) (t := t).
      * exact Hs.
      * exact Hlc.
      * exact Hbody.
      * apply R_step with (t := u). exact Hu. exact Hs.
  - apply value_app_inv.
Qed.
```
Wait, the IH form: IH : forall u', u --> u' -> forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> ... -> R T1 u' -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) u'). So `apply IH with (T1 := T1) (T2 := T2) (t := t)` leaves goals: `u --> u'` (Hs), `lc_at 1 t`, `forall v, ...`, `R T1 u'`. Order might differ. Let me instead write `exact (IH u' Hs T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody (R_step T1 u u' Hu Hs))`. That's cleaner if the argument order matches. But the exact IH order depends on how Coq generalizes. Let me be adaptive during compilation.

Hmm, one important detail: after `induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH]`, does the motive include T1 T2 t Hlc Hbody (i.e., are they introduced before or after)? I do `intros u Hsn. induction Hsn as [u Hreds IH].` — at that point the goal is `forall T1 T2 t, ...` and T1 T2 t are NOT introduced. So the motive is `fun x => forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> ... -> R T1 x -> R T2 (app (abs T1 t) x)` — u is generalized. Then IH : forall u', u --> u' -> forall T1 T2 t, lc_at 1 t -> ... . Good. Now, in the AppAbs case: after inversion, the equations should give T0 = T1, body = t, v = u, r = open body v. `subst` should handle. Then goal SN r → becomes SN (open t u) (after substs). Hbody u Hu : R T2 (open t u) ✓.

Now fundamental:

```coq
Definition valuation (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)).

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, valuation Gamma sigma -> R T (subst_fvar sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T HT.
  induction HT as [Gamma x T Hx
                | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
                | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                | Gamma
                | Gamma];
  intros sigma [Hval1 Hval2].
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hval1. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *) ...
  - (* T_App *) simpl. pose proof (IH1 sigma (conj Hval1 Hval2)) as R1.
    pose proof (IH2 sigma (conj Hval1 Hval2)) as R2.
    exact (R_app T1 T2 (subst_fvar sigma t1) (subst_fvar sigma t2) R1 R2).
  - (* True *) simpl. split. apply lc_true. apply SN_no_step. intros t' H. exact (step_true_inv t' H).
  - (* False *) simpl. split. apply lc_false. apply SN_no_step. intros t' H. exact (step_false_inv t' H).
Qed.
```

Hmm, careful in T_Var: after `simpl`, goal R T (subst_fvar sigma (tm_fvar x)) → R T (sigma x). `simpl` should reduce ✓.

In T_App: goal R T2 (subst_fvar sigma (tm_app t1 t2)); simpl → R T2 (tm_app (subst_fvar sigma t1) (subst_fvar sigma t2)) ✓.

In T_True: R Ty_Bool (subst_fvar sigma tm_true) → R Ty_Bool tm_true = lc ∧ SN (after simpl). ✓

Actually, regarding inversion patterns: let me write `step_app_inv` more defensively:

```coq
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t u r,
  tm_app t u --> r ->
  (exists T body v, t = tm_abs T body /\ value t /\ u = v /\ r = open body v) \/
  (exists t', t --> t' /\ r = tm_app t' u) \/
  (exists u', value t /\ u --> u' /\ r = tm_app t u').
Proof.
  intros t u r H. inversion H; subst.
  - left. exists T, t0, v. repeat split; try reflexivity. apply v_abs. assumption.
  - right; left. exists t1'. split; assumption.
  - right; right. exists t2'. repeat split; try assumption; try reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Let me now also double check the `step_app_inv` usage in `Red_nonvalue`: The destruct pattern must match the disjunction structure:
`A \/ B \/ C` parses as `A \/ (B \/ C)`. Pattern: `[[T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] | [[t' [Hs Er]] | [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]]]]`.
- A = exists T body v, t = tm_abs T body /\ value t /\ u = v /\ r = open body v. Parsing: `exists T, exists body, exists v, (t = ... /\ value t /\ u = v /\ r = ...)`. The conjunction is right-assoc: t = abs /\ (value t /\ (u = v /\ r = open body v)). Pattern: [T0 [body [v [Et [Hv [Eu Er]]]]]] → Et : t = abs, Hv : value t, Eu : u = v, Er : r = open body v ✓.
- B = exists t', t --> t' /\ r = tm_app t' u: [t' [Hs Er]] ✓.
- C = exists u', value t /\ u --> u' /\ r = tm_app t u': [u' [Hv [Hs Er]]] ✓.

```coq
Lemma has_type_ind' : forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
  (forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (tm_fvar x) T) ->
  (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
